You are the second reviewer on a routine security code review of our own
nix-bitcoin fork: the NixOS modules and packaging we deploy on our own Bitcoin
and Lightning node. Two prior reviewers have already produced the findings
below against the exact commit checked out in the current directory. Your job
is quality control on their work, as the maintainer's independent check:

For EACH finding, open the cited file at the cited location and decide:
- verdict: "confirmed" (the defect is real as described), "false-positive"
  (the code does not do what the finding claims, or an existing control
  already prevents it — name the control), or "needs-info" (cannot be decided
  from the tree; say what is missing).
- severity: your own call (critical/high/medium/low/info), which may differ
  from the reviewer's.
- reasoning: one to three sentences grounded in the code you read (quote the
  line or construct that decides it).
- fix_ok: whether the reviewer's recommended fix is correct and complete
  (true/false) and, if false, what is wrong with it.

Then, only for the files the findings touch, note up to three defects the
reviewers missed or misread that a maintainer should know about ("missed").
Do not pad: if there is nothing, say so.

Read-only tools only; do not modify the tree. Be terse and concrete.

End your answer with exactly one fenced ```json block of this shape:
{"reviews":[{"id":"<finding id or title>","verdict":"confirmed|false-positive|needs-info","severity":"...","reasoning":"...","fix_ok":true,"fix_note":""}],
 "missed":[{"file":"...","line":0,"severity":"...","title":"...","description":"..."}],
 "summary":"two or three sentences"}
followed by nothing else.

    "description": "The script verifies the release nar-hash with the one-argument form `gpg --verify nar-hash.txt.asc`. gpg then picks the verification mode from the packet structure: for a non-detached (inline/cleartext) signature it verifies the payload embedded in the .asc and never reads nar-hash.txt, exiting 0 while printing only a warning, which `&> /dev/null` hides. The script then emits the unverified contents of nar-hash.txt as a 'verified' fetchTarball pin. Reproduced empirically on this machine: an inline-signed message of unrelated content plus an attacker-written nar-hash.txt yields exit 0; upstream's two-argument form correctly exits 2. This is exactly the bug upstream fort-nix fixed in da27f27 ('fetch-release: fix signature verification!', 2026-08-31) after the fork point (37931e5, 2026-08-13); the fork edited this file for its NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM guard but kept the broken primitive. Exploitation requires the victim to set NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1 and an attacker who can write release assets on the archived fort-nix repo plus any inline/cleartext-signed artifact from the release key; impact is a user pinning a malicious tarball with a 'verified' label.",

# lean-ctx — Context Engineering for AI Agents

## Setup

```bash
which lean-ctx || curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yvgude/lean-ctx/main/skills/lean-ctx/scripts/install.sh | bash
lean-ctx setup
```

## Core Tools (10 always visible)

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `ctx_read(path, mode)` | Read file with compression and caching |
| `ctx_search(pattern, path)` | Search code with compressed results |
| `ctx_shell(command)` | Run shell with compressed output |
| `ctx_tree(path, depth)` | Directory listing |
| `ctx_patch(path, ops)` | Anchored editing (line+hash, no old-text echo) |
| `ctx_session(action)` | Session state and persistence |
| `ctx_knowledge(action)` | Project knowledge across sessions |
| `ctx_overview(task)` | Task-relevant project map |
| `ctx_graph(action)` | Code relationships and impact |
| `ctx_call(name, args)` | Invoke any tool by name |

## Shell Hook (use instead of raw exec)

```bash
lean-ctx -c "git status"
lean-ctx -c "cargo test"
lean-ctx -c "npm install"
lean-ctx ls src/
```

## ctx_read Modes

| Mode | When |
|------|------|
| `anchored` | Files you will edit (full text + `N:hh\|` anchors for ctx_patch) |
| `full` | Verbatim cached read |
| `map` | Context-only (deps + exports) |
| `signatures` | API surface only |
| `diff` | After edits (changed lines) |
| `aggressive` | Large files, syntax-stripped; JSON arrays row-deduped (lossless) |
| `entropy` | Shannon filtering |
| `task` | Task-relevant lines |
| `lines:N-M` | Specific range |
| `auto` | System selects optimal |

Re-reads cost ~13 tokens. fresh=true bypasses cache.
Redundant JSON (arrays of like objects) is crushed losslessly into a compact
`_defaults` + per-row form; if a slice was dropped, recover it with
`ctx_expand(id, json_path=… | search=…)`.

## File Editing

Anchored editing saves output tokens: `ctx_read(mode="anchored")` → `ctx_patch(path, op, line, hash, new_text)`.
Never reproduce old text byte-for-byte; batch via `ops:[…]`; `op=create` writes new files.
Stale anchor → CONFLICT with fresh anchors (retry once). Native Edit/StrReplace stay fine;
`ctx_edit` (str_replace) is the legacy fallback via ctx_call/power profile.

## More Tools (via ctx_call or ctx_load_tools)

Architecture: ctx_symbol, ctx_callgraph, ctx_impact, ctx_architecture, ctx_routes, ctx_smells, ctx_quality
  ↳ "What breaks if I change this file/class/type?" → ctx_impact (file-level blast radius; resolves same-package/namespace type usage with no import for C#, Java, Go and Kotlin). "Who calls this function?" → ctx_callgraph (symbol-level). "How navigable / how much is complexity costing me?" → ctx_quality (navigability score + token quality tax).
Multi-agent: ctx_agent, ctx_share, ctx_task, ctx_handoff, ctx_workflow
Verify: ctx_benchmark, ctx_verify, ctx_proof, ctx_review
Batch: ctx_fill, ctx_execute, ctx_expand, ctx_pack

Full docs: https://leanctx.com/docs

     1	{ config, pkgs, lib, ... }:

     3	# Create a WireGuard server with a single peer.
     4	# Private/public keys are created via the secrets system.
     5	# Add helper binaries `nix-bitcoin-wg-connect` and optionally `lndconnect-wg`.
     6	
     7	# See ../../docs/services.md ("Use Zeus (mobile lightning wallet) via WireGuard")
     8	# for usage instructions.
     9	
    10	# This is a rather opinionated implementation that lacks the flexibility offered by
    11	# other nix-bitcoin modules, so ship this as a `preset`.
    12	# Some users will prefer to use `lndconnect` with their existing WireGuard or Tailscale setup.
    13	
    14	with lib;
    15	let
    16	  options.nix-bitcoin.wireguard = {
    17	    subnet = mkOption {
    18	      type = types.str;
    19	      default = "10.10.0";
    20	      description = "The /24 subnet of the wireguard network.";
    21	    };
    22	    restrictPeer = mkOption {
    23	      type = types.bool;
    24	      default = true;
    25	      description = ''
    26	        Prevent the peer from connecting to any addresses except for the WireGuard server address.
    27	      '';
    28	    };
    29	  };
    30	
    31	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.wireguard;
    32	  wgSubnet = cfg.subnet;
    33	  inherit (config.networking.wireguard.interfaces) wg-nb;
    34	  inherit (config.services) lnd;
    35	
    36	  lndconnect = lnd.enable && lnd.lndconnect.enable;
    37	
    38	  serverAddress = "${wgSubnet}.1";
    39	  peerAddress = "${wgSubnet}.2";
    40	
    41	  secretsDir = config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir;
    42	
    43	  wgConnectUser = if config.nix-bitcoin.operator.enable
    44	                  then config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name
    45	                  else "root";
    46	
    47	  # A script that prints a QR code to connect a peer to the server.
    48	  # The QR code encodes a wg-quick config that can be imported by the wireguard
    49	  # mobile app.
    50	  wgConnect = pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "nix-bitcoin-wg-connect" ''
    51	    set -euo pipefail
    52	    text=
    53	    host=
    54	    for arg in "$@"; do
    55	      case $arg in
    56	        --text)
    57	          text=1
    58	          ;;
    59	        *)
    60	          host=$arg
    61	          ;;
    62	      esac
    63	    done
    64	
    65	    if [[ ! $host ]]; then
    66	      # Use lndconnect to fetch the external ip.
    67	      # This internally uses https://github.com/GlenDC/go-external-ip, which
    68	      # queries a set of external ip providers.
    69	      host=$(
    70	        ${getExe config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.lndconnect} --url --nocert \
    71	          --configfile=/dev/null --adminmacaroonpath=/dev/null \
    72	          | sed -nE 's|.*?/(.*?):.*|\1|p'
    73	      )
    74	    fi
    75	
    76	    config="[Interface]
    77	    PrivateKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-peer-private-key)
    78	    Address = ${peerAddress}/24
    79	
    80	    [Peer]
    81	    PublicKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-server-public-key)
    82	    AllowedIPs = ${wgSubnet}.0/24
    83	    Endpoint = $host:${toString wg-nb.listenPort}
    84	    PersistentKeepalive = 25
    85	    "
    86	
    87	    if [[ $text ]]; then
    88	      echo "$config"
    89	    else
    90	      echo "$config" | ${getExe pkgs.qrencode} -t UTF8 -o -
    91	    fi
    92	  '';
    93	in {
    94	  inherit options;
    95	
    96	  config = {
    97	    assertions = [
    98	      {
    99	        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
   100	        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
   101	        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
   102	      }
   103	      # The whole security model of this preset is the iptables rule set
   104	      # below: the lnd admin REST listener is bound to 0.0.0.0 (see the comment
   105	      # at `restAddress`) and the peer restriction is an iptables REJECT. Two
   106	      # silent ways to lose that (audit 2026-09-09): the firewall disabled, or
   107	      # the nftables backend enabled — NixOS then ignores `extraCommands`
   108	      # without an error, so the REST accept rule and the restrictPeer REJECT

   115	      }
   116	      {
   117	        # firewalld replaces the iptables backend the same way nftables does
   118	        # (audit second opinion 2026-10-01).
   119	        assertion = !(config.networking.nftables.enable || (config.services.firewalld.enable or false));
   120	        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: its rules are iptables `extraCommands`, which the nftables backend (networking.nftables.enable) and firewalld (services.firewalld.enable) silently drop. Keep the iptables backend or port the rules.";
   121	      }
   122	    ];
   123	
   124	    networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb = {
   125	      ips = [ "${serverAddress}/24" ];
   126	      listenPort = mkDefault 51820;
   127	      privateKeyFile = "${secretsDir}/wg-server-private-key";
   128	      allowedIPsAsRoutes = false;
   129	      peers = [
   130	        {
   131	          # To use the actual public key from the secrets file, use dummy pubkey
   132	          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
   133	          # at peer service runtime.
   134	          publicKey = "peer0";
   135	          allowedIPs = [ "${peerAddress}/32" ];
   136	        }
   137	      ];
   138	    };
   139	
   140	    systemd.services = {
   141	      wireguard-wg-nb = rec {
   142	        wants = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   143	        after = wants;
   144	      };
   145	
   146	      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
   147	      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
   148	      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
   149	      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
   150	        script = mkForce ''
   151	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" allowed-ips ${peerAddress}/32
   152	        '';
   153	        postStop = mkForce ''
   154	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" remove || true
   155	        '';
   156	      };
   157	    };
   158	
   159	    environment.systemPackages = [
   160	      wgConnect
   161	    ] ++ (optional lndconnect
   162	      (pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "lndconnect-wg" ''
   163	        exec lndconnect --host "${serverAddress}" --nocert "$@"
   164	      '')
   165	    );
   166	
   167	    networking.firewall = let
   168	      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
   169	    in {
   170	      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
   171	
   172	      extraCommands =
   173	        optionalString lndconnect ''
   174	          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
   175	          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
   176	          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
   177	          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
   178	          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
   179	          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
   180	        ''
   181	        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
   182	          # Insert at the top: appended (-A) the REJECT would sit behind the
   183	          # standard accept rules (allowed ports, the REST accept above), so a
   184	          # peer could still reach any firewall-allowed port on the node's
   185	          # other addresses through the tunnel (second opinion 2026-09-09).
   186	          # Replies to server-initiated traffic are unaffected: their
   187	          # destination is serverAddress.
   188	          iptables -w -I nixos-fw 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   189	          iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   190	        '';
   191	
   192	      extraStopCommands =
   193	        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
   194	        # the NixOS firewall service.
   195	        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
   196	          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
   197	        '';
   198	    };
   199	
   200	    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
   201	    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
   202	    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
   203	    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
   204	    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
   205	    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
   206	    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
   207	    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
   208	    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
   209	    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
   210	    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
   211	      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
   212	      tor.enforce = false;
   213	    };
   214	
   215	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   216	      wg-server-private-key = {};
   217	      wg-server-public-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   218	      wg-peer-private-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   219	      wg-peer-public-key = {};
   220	    };
   221	
   222	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.wireguard = let
   223	      wg = "${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg";
   224	    in ''
   225	      makeWireguardKey() {
   226	        local name=$1
   227	        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
   228	        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
   229	        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
   230	          ${wg} genkey > $priv
   231	        fi
   232	        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
   233	          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
   234	        fi
   235	      }
   236	      makeWireguardKey server
   237	      makeWireguardKey peer
   238	    '';
   239	  };
   240	}

     1	# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
     2	
     3	Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
     4	
     5	The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
     6	that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
     7	(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
     8	interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
     9	accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
    10	The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
    11	10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
    12	things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
    13	
    14	> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
    15	> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
    16	> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
    17	> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
    18	
    19	## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
    20	
    21	1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
    22	   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
    23	   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
    24	   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
    25	   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
    26	   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
    27	   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
    28	   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
    29	   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
    30	   auditor trade-off of its own).
    31	
    32	2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
    33	   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
    34	   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
    35	   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
    36	   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
    37	   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
    38	   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
    39	   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
    40	   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
    41	   must tolerate rotation.
    42	
    43	3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
    44	   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
    45	   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
    46	   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
    47	   the daemon is healthy.
    48	
    49	4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
    50	   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
    51	   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
    52	   wg-dependent.
    53	
    54	5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
    55	   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
    56	   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
    57	
    58	6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
    59	   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
    60	
    61	## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
    62	
    63	Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
    64	the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
    65	entries:
    66	
    67	```nix
    68	services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
    69	  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
    70	'';
    71	```
    72	
    73	All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
    74	wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
    75	targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
    76	dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
    77	
    78	- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
    79	  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
    80	  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
    81	- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
    82	  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
    83	  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
    84	  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
    85	  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
    86	
    87	## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
    88	
    89	- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
    90	- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
    91	      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
    92	      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
    93	      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
    94	      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
    95	      connects.
    96	- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
    97	- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
    98	      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
    99	      existing clients.
   100	- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
   101	      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
   102	      items 1–6.

     1	# RPC calls that are safe for public use.
     2	#
     3	# Safety bar: bounded CPU cost per call, no P2P side effects beyond
     4	# transaction relay, no leaks of peer topology or wallet contents.
     5	#
     6	# Deliberately excluded (audit M-3):
     7	# - scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate: minutes-scale CPU,
     8	#   trivially abused for DoS by any holder of the public credentials.
     9	# - getblockfrompeer: lets the caller drive the node's P2P block fetching.
    10	# - fundrawtransaction: operates on a loaded bitcoind wallet — it selects and
    11	#   locks UTXOs and reveals change/wallet contents, violating the wallet-safety
    12	#   bar above. No local consumer (lnd, electrs, nbxplorer) uses it; lnd/nbxplorer
    13	#   manage their own wallets and electrs only reads blocks. Removed in the audit
    14	#   follow-up 2026-08-20.
    15	# If you need any of these, grant them via
    16	# `services.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist` in your own config.
    17	#
    18	# NOTE: getpeerinfo / getnodeaddresses stay in the list. This "public" user
    19	# is also the default RPC user that LOCAL services use, and lnd REQUIRES both
    20	# (its chain-backend health check + pruned-mode block fetching) — removing
    21	# them makes lnd fail its health check and shut itself down. They only leak
    22	# peer topology if you actually expose this user over a public proxy; if you
    23	# do that, narrow the whitelist in your own config rather than here.

     1	/* This program requires CAP_SYS_ADMIN */
     2	
     3	#define _GNU_SOURCE
     4	#include <sched.h>
     5	#include <stdio.h>
     6	#include <errno.h>
     7	#include <string.h>
     8	#include <unistd.h>
     9	#include <fcntl.h>
    10	#include <sys/capability.h>
    11	
    12	/* Netns names of the services this fork ships (see modules/netns-isolation.nix) */
    13	static char *allowed_netns[] = {
    14	    "nb-bitcoind",
    15	    "nb-lnd",
    16	    "nb-electrs",
    17	    "nb-nbxplorer",
    18	    "nb-btcpayserver"
    19	};
    20	
    21	int is_netns_allowed(char *netns) {
    22	    int n_allowed_netns = sizeof(allowed_netns) / sizeof(allowed_netns[0]);
    23	    for (int i = 0; i < n_allowed_netns; i++) {
    24	        if (strcmp(allowed_netns[i], netns) == 0) {
    25	            return 1;
    26	        }
    27	    }
    28	    return 0;
    29	}
    30	
    31	void print_capabilities() {
    32	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    33	    printf("Capabilities: %s\n", cap_to_text(caps, NULL));
    34	    cap_free(caps);
    35	}
    36	
    37	int drop_capabilities() {
    38	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    39	    if (caps == NULL) return -1;
    40	    if (cap_clear(caps) < 0) { cap_free(caps); return -1; }
    41	    int rc = cap_set_proc(caps);
    42	    cap_free(caps);
    43	    return rc;
    44	}
    45	
    46	int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    47	    char netns_path[256];
    48	
    49	    if (argc < 3) {
    50	        printf("usage: %s <netns> <command>\n", argv[0]);
    51	        return 1;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    if (!is_netns_allowed(argv[1])) {
    55	        printf("%s is not an allowed netns.\n", argv[1]);
    56	        return 1;
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    /* Require an absolute path so PATH cannot redirect the exec target.
    60	       The caller's environment is otherwise inherited; the operator who may
    61	       run this wrapper is already root-equivalent. */
    62	    if (argv[2][0] != '/') {
    63	        printf("command must be an absolute path.\n");
    64	        return 1;
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    int len = snprintf(netns_path, sizeof(netns_path), "/var/run/netns/%s", argv[1]);
    68	    if (len < 0 || (size_t)len >= sizeof(netns_path)) {
    69	        printf("Path length exceeded for netns %s.\n", argv[1]);
    70	        return 1;
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    int fd = open(netns_path, O_RDONLY);
    74	    if (fd < 0) {
    75	        printf("Failed opening netns %s: %d, %s \n", netns_path, errno, strerror(errno));
    76	        return 1;
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    if (setns(fd, CLONE_NEWNET) < 0) {
    80	        printf("Failed setns %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    81	        close(fd);
    82	        return 1;
    83	    }
    84	    close(fd);
    85	
    86	    /* Drop capabilities */
    87	    #ifdef DEBUG
    88	    print_capabilities();
    89	    #endif
    90	    if (drop_capabilities() < 0) {
    91	        printf("Failed to drop capabilities: %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    92	        return 1;
    93	    }
    94	    #ifdef DEBUG
    95	    print_capabilities();
    96	    #endif
    97	
    98	    execv(argv[2], &argv[2]);
    99	    /* Only reached on exec failure */
   100	    printf("Failed to exec %s: %d, %s \n", argv[2], errno, strerror(errno));
   101	    return 1;
   102	}

     1	#!/usr/bin/env nix-shell
     2	#!nix-shell -i bash -p bash coreutils curl jq gnupg gnugrep
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	
     5	scriptDir=$(cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}" && pwd)
     6	
     7	trap 'echo "Error at ${BASH_SOURCE[0]}:$LINENO"' ERR
     8	
     9	repo=fort-nix/nix-bitcoin
    10	
    11	# This legacy helper fetches an UPSTREAM (fort-nix) signed release tarball. This
    12	# repository is a maintained FORK; upstream's release artifacts do NOT contain
    13	# this fork's fixes, so fetching them here would silently ship un-fixed code.
    14	# Consume this fork via its flake pin instead:
    15	#   inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = "github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release";
    16	# Refuse to run unless explicitly overridden.
    17	if [[ ! -v NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM ]]; then
    18	    echo "This is a fork of nix-bitcoin; pin the flake, don't fetch releases:" >&2
    19	    echo "  inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = \"github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release\";" >&2
    20	    echo "This helper fetches UPSTREAM ($repo) releases, WITHOUT the fork's fixes." >&2
    21	    echo "Set NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1 only if you truly want upstream." >&2
    22	    exit 1
    23	fi
    24	
    25	if [[ ! -v version ]]; then
    26	    version=$(curl -fsS "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo/releases/latest" | jq -r '.tag_name' | tail -c +2)
    27	fi
    28	
    29	TMPDIR=$(mktemp -d)
    30	trap 'rm -rf $TMPDIR' EXIT
    31	
    32	export GNUPGHOME=$TMPDIR/gpg-home
    33	mkdir -m 700 "$GNUPGHOME"
    34	
    35	# Import key
    36	gpg --import "$scriptDir/key-jonasnick.bin" &> /dev/null
    37	# Check that exactly one key was imported
    38	(($(gpg --list-keys --with-colons | grep -c pub) == 1))
    39	# Verify key fingerprint
    40	gpg --list-keys "36C7 1A37 C9D9 88BD E825 08D9 B1A7 0E4F 8DCD 0366" > /dev/null
    41	
    42	# Fetch nar-hash of release
    43	cd "$TMPDIR"
    44	baseUrl=https://github.com/$repo/releases/download/v$version
    45	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt"
    46	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt.asc"
    47	
    48	# Verify signature for nar-hash
    49	gpg --verify nar-hash.txt.asc &> /dev/null || {
    50	    >&2 echo "Error: Signature verification failed. Please open an issue in the project repository."
    51	    exit 1
    52	}
    53	
    54	>&2 echo "Fetched and verified release $version"
    55	
    56	cat <<EOF
    57	builtins.fetchTarball {
    58	  url = "https://github.com/$repo/archive/v$version.tar.gz";
    59	  sha256 = "$(cat nar-hash.txt)";
    60	}
    61	EOF
     1	# Security Policy
     2	
     3	This repository is an independently maintained fork of
     4	[fort-nix/nix-bitcoin](https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin), which is no
     5	longer actively maintained. The contacts, GPG keys, signing key, and security
     6	fund listed in upstream's SECURITY.md belong to the original developers and do
     7	not reach or represent this fork. Do not send reports or donations to them on
     8	our behalf.
     9	
    10	## Reporting a Vulnerability
    11	
    12	Report vulnerabilities through [GitHub private vulnerability
    13	reporting](../../security/advisories/new). This opens a private security
    14	advisory that only the maintainers can see.
    15	
    16	If private reporting is unavailable, open a regular issue that says you have a
    17	security concern, without any details, and we will arrange a private channel.
    18	
    19	Please include:
    20	
    21	- The component and version or commit you tested
    22	- Steps to reproduce or a proof of concept
    23	- The impact you see (fund theft, privacy loss, denial of service, ...)
    24	
    25	We aim to acknowledge reports within 7 days. We follow coordinated disclosure:
    26	we agree on a public disclosure date with you, credit you in the fix and
    27	release notes unless you prefer anonymity.
    28	
    29	## Scope
    30	
    31	In scope:
    32	
    33	- nix-bitcoin's own code: NixOS modules, `netns-exec`, secrets handling,
    34	  helper scripts, packaging in `pkgs/`
    35	- Privilege boundaries we claim to provide: service isolation, RPC
    36	  whitelisting, `netns-isolation`, systemd sandboxing
    37	- Documentation that instructs users to do something blatantly insecure
    38	
    39	Out of scope:
    40	
    41	- Vulnerabilities in the daemons we package (bitcoind, lnd,
    42	  electrs, btcpayserver, nbxplorer, ...). Report those to the upstream
    43	  project first. If upstream has shipped a fix that we have not yet pulled in,
    44	  tell us and we will treat it as urgent.
    45	- Attacks requiring physical access or an already-compromised deployment
    46	  machine.
    47	
    48	## Supported Versions
    49	
    50	Only the latest commit on the default branch and the latest release are
    51	supported. Security fixes are delivered by updating your flake input; we do
    52	not maintain stable branches.
    53	
    54	## Security Tracker
    55	
    56	Known findings and their status are tracked in
    57	[docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md](docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md). The
    58	audit is re-run on every nixpkgs pin update.
    59	
    60	## Adversarial review
    61	
    62	Beyond CI's NixOS VM assertion suite, every change is reviewed by two
    63	independent open-weight models (Kimi K3 + GLM-5.2) acting as adversarial
    64	security auditors, at max reasoning. The **methodology, the audit tooling, and
    65	the full results of every run** live on the [`audits`](../../tree/audits)
    66	branch — kept off `master`/`release` so the code you pin stays clean. Browse it
    67	to follow the reviews, read the raw transcripts, or run the suite yourself;
    68	PRs to improve the audit prompt are welcome.
    69	
    70	## Release Integrity
    71	
    72	Commits on this fork are signed with the maintainer's SSH key. GitHub shows
    73	them as "Verified"; to verify locally, add the maintainer's public signing key
    74	to an SSH allowed-signers file and run `git log --show-signature` or
    75	`git verify-commit <rev>`. The CalVer release tags are created by CI
    76	(`release-tag.yml`) as annotated but **unsigned** tags — a tag name is a
    77	convenience pointer, not a signature. Verify the *commit* a tag points at, and
    78	pin by commit hash + `narHash` (below) rather than by tag name.
    79	
    80	Because this fork is consumed as a flake input, integrity ultimately comes
    81	from your `flake.lock`: it records the exact commit and its content hash
    82	(`narHash`), and Nix refuses any input whose contents do not match. Pin a
    83	specific revision and review changes before advancing it.
    84	
    85	`bitcoind` source tarballs are additionally verified against the Bitcoin
    86	Core `guix.sigs` keyring at build time.
    87	
    88	Note: the upstream tarball-release helper `helper/fetch-release` is not used by
    89	this fork. It fetches upstream fort-nix releases and references the original
    90	maintainer's GPG key. It refuses to run unless `NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1`
    91	is set, and it points at the flake pin instead. Do not rely on it. This fork
    92	releases through CalVer tags on the `release` branch (`release-tag.yml`).

codex
The signature check uses the one-argument GPG form, and the signing claim is present in SECURITY.md. I also found a flaw in FW-4’s proposed fix: `netns-exec` requires an absolute executable path, so the suggested `capsh` command would fail before testing capability dropping.

/bin/bash -lc "lean-ctx -c \"rg -n '"'^'"' test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md\"" in /home/dan/fork-audit/src-so-2026-10-04-072616
 succeeded in 350ms:
1:{ config, pkgs, lib, ... }:
2:
3:# Create a WireGuard server with a single peer.
4:# Private/public keys are created via the secrets system.
5:# Add helper binaries `nix-bitcoin-wg-connect` and optionally `lndconnect-wg`.
6:
7:# See ../../docs/services.md ("Use Zeus (mobile lightning wallet) via WireGuard")
8:# for usage instructions.
9:
10:# This is a rather opinionated implementation that lacks the flexibility offered by
11:# other nix-bitcoin modules, so ship this as a `preset`.
12:# Some users will prefer to use `lndconnect` with their existing WireGuard or Tailscale setup.
13:
14:with lib;
15:let
16:  options.nix-bitcoin.wireguard = {
17:    subnet = mkOption {
18:      type = types.str;
19:      default = "10.10.0";
20:      description = "The /24 subnet of the wireguard network.";
21:    };
22:    restrictPeer = mkOption {
23:      type = types.bool;
24:      default = true;
25:      description = ''
26:        Prevent the peer from connecting to any addresses except for the WireGuard server address.
27:      '';
28:    };
29:  };
30:
31:  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.wireguard;
32:  wgSubnet = cfg.subnet;
33:  inherit (config.networking.wireguard.interfaces) wg-nb;
34:  inherit (config.services) lnd;
35:
36:  lndconnect = lnd.enable && lnd.lndconnect.enable;
37:
38:  serverAddress = "${wgSubnet}.1";
39:  peerAddress = "${wgSubnet}.2";
40:
41:  secretsDir = config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir;
42:
43:  wgConnectUser = if config.nix-bitcoin.operator.enable
44:                  then config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name
45:                  else "root";
46:
47:  # A script that prints a QR code to connect a peer to the server.
48:  # The QR code encodes a wg-quick config that can be imported by the wireguard
49:  # mobile app.
50:  wgConnect = pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "nix-bitcoin-wg-connect" ''
51:    set -euo pipefail
52:    text=
53:    host=
54:    for arg in "$@"; do
55:      case $arg in
56:        --text)
57:          text=1
58:          ;;
59:        *)
60:          host=$arg
61:          ;;
62:      esac
63:    done
64:
65:    if [[ ! $host ]]; then
66:      # Use lndconnect to fetch the external ip.
67:      # This internally uses https://github.com/GlenDC/go-external-ip, which
68:      # queries a set of external ip providers.
69:      host=$(
70:        ${getExe config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.lndconnect} --url --nocert \
71:          --configfile=/dev/null --adminmacaroonpath=/dev/null \
72:          | sed -nE 's|.*?/(.*?):.*|\1|p'
73:      )
74:    fi
75:
76:    config="[Interface]
77:    PrivateKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-peer-private-key)
78:    Address = ${peerAddress}/24
79:
80:    [Peer]
81:    PublicKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-server-public-key)
82:    AllowedIPs = ${wgSubnet}.0/24
83:    Endpoint = $host:${toString wg-nb.listenPort}
84:    PersistentKeepalive = 25
85:    "
86:
87:    if [[ $text ]]; then
88:      echo "$config"
89:    else
90:      echo "$config" | ${getExe pkgs.qrencode} -t UTF8 -o -
91:    fi
92:  '';
93:in {
94:  inherit options;
95:
96:  config = {
97:    assertions = [
98:      {
99:        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
100:        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
101:        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
102:      }
103:      # The whole security model of this preset is the iptables rule set
104:      # below: the lnd admin REST listener is bound to 0.0.0.0 (see the comment
105:      # at `restAddress`) and the peer restriction is an iptables REJECT. Two
106:      # silent ways to lose that (audit 2026-09-09): the firewall disabled, or
107:      # the nftables backend enabled — NixOS then ignores `extraCommands`
108:      # without an error, so the REST accept rule and the restrictPeer REJECT
109:      # simply vanish. Refuse to evaluate rather than ship an unguarded admin API.
110:      {
111:        # restrictPeer is also only an iptables REJECT: with the firewall off it
112:        # evaluates fine and installs nothing (audit second opinion 2026-10-01).
113:        assertion = !(lndconnect || cfg.restrictPeer) || config.networking.firewall.enable;
114:        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: services.lnd.lndconnect binds the admin REST API to 0.0.0.0 and `restrictPeer` is an iptables REJECT; both rely on networking.firewall — enable the firewall.";
115:      }
116:      {
117:        # firewalld replaces the iptables backend the same way nftables does
118:        # (audit second opinion 2026-10-01).
119:        assertion = !(config.networking.nftables.enable || (config.services.firewalld.enable or false));
120:        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: its rules are iptables `extraCommands`, which the nftables backend (networking.nftables.enable) and firewalld (services.firewalld.enable) silently drop. Keep the iptables backend or port the rules.";
121:      }
122:    ];
123:
124:    networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb = {
125:      ips = [ "${serverAddress}/24" ];
126:      listenPort = mkDefault 51820;
127:      privateKeyFile = "${secretsDir}/wg-server-private-key";
128:      allowedIPsAsRoutes = false;
129:      peers = [
130:        {
131:          # To use the actual public key from the secrets file, use dummy pubkey
132:          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
133:          # at peer service runtime.
134:          publicKey = "peer0";
135:          allowedIPs = [ "${peerAddress}/32" ];
136:        }
137:      ];
138:    };
139:
140:    systemd.services = {
141:      wireguard-wg-nb = rec {
142:        wants = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
143:        after = wants;
144:      };
145:
146:      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
147:      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
148:      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
149:      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
150:        script = mkForce ''
151:          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" allowed-ips ${peerAddress}/32
152:        '';
153:        postStop = mkForce ''
154:          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" remove || true
155:        '';
156:      };
157:    };
158:
159:    environment.systemPackages = [
160:      wgConnect
161:    ] ++ (optional lndconnect
162:      (pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "lndconnect-wg" ''
163:        exec lndconnect --host "${serverAddress}" --nocert "$@"
164:      '')
165:    );
166:
167:    networking.firewall = let
168:      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
169:    in {
170:      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
171:
172:      extraCommands =
173:        optionalString lndconnect ''
174:          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
175:          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
176:          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
177:          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
178:          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
179:          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
180:        ''
181:        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
182:          # Insert at the top: appended (-A) the REJECT would sit behind the
183:          # standard accept rules (allowed ports, the REST accept above), so a
184:          # peer could still reach any firewall-allowed port on the node's
185:          # other addresses through the tunnel (second opinion 2026-09-09).
186:          # Replies to server-initiated traffic are unaffected: their
187:          # destination is serverAddress.
188:          iptables -w -I nixos-fw 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
189:          iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
190:        '';
191:
192:      extraStopCommands =
193:        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
194:        # the NixOS firewall service.
195:        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
196:          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
197:        '';
198:    };
199:
200:    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
201:    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
202:    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
203:    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
204:    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
205:    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
206:    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
207:    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
208:    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
209:    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
210:    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
211:      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
212:      tor.enforce = false;
213:    };
214:
215:    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
216:      wg-server-private-key = {};
217:      wg-server-public-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
218:      wg-peer-private-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
219:      wg-peer-public-key = {};
220:    };
221:
222:    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.wireguard = let
223:      wg = "${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg";
224:    in ''
225:      makeWireguardKey() {
226:        local name=$1
227:        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
228:        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
229:        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
230:          ${wg} genkey > $priv
231:        fi
232:        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
233:          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
234:        fi
235:      }
236:      makeWireguardKey server
237:      makeWireguardKey peer
238:    '';

def _():
    assert_running("onion-addresses")

# Run this test before the following tests that shut down services
# (and their corresponding network namespaces).
@test("netns-isolation")
def _():
    def get_ips(services):
        enabled = enabled_tests.intersection(services)
        return " ".join(ip(service) for service in enabled)

    def assert_reachable(src, dests):
        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
            machine.succeed(f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 {dest_ips}")

    def assert_unreachable(src, dests):
        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
            machine.fail(
                # This fails when no host is reachable within 100 ms
                f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 --reachable=1 {dest_ips}"
            )

    # These reachability tests are non-exhaustive
    assert_reachable("bitcoind", ["clightning", "lnd", "liquidd"])
    assert_unreachable("bitcoind", ["btcpayserver", "rtl", "lightning-loop"])
    assert_unreachable("btcpayserver", ["bitcoind", "lightning-loop"])

    # netns addresses can not be bound to in the main netns.
    # This prevents processes in the main netns from impersonating nix-bitcoin services.
    assert_matches(
        f"nc -l {ip('bitcoind')} 1080 2>&1 || true", "nc: Cannot assign requested address"
    )

    # netns-exec should fail for unauthorized namespaces
    assert_matches(
        "runuser -u operator -- netns-exec nb-clightning ip a 2>&1 || true",
        "nb-clightning is not an allowed netns",
    )

    # netns-exec should only be executable by the operator user.
    # User `unauthorized` is a member of group `users`, like all normal users.
    # Netns `nb-clightning` is rejected by netns-exec before it accesses the
    # netns, so the error below can only originate from the exec permissions.
    assert_matches(
        "runuser -u unauthorized -- netns-exec nb-clightning ip a 2>&1 || true",
        "Permission denied",
    )

    if "joinmarket" in enabled_tests:
        # netns-exec should drop capabilities
        assert_matches(
            "runuser -u operator -- netns-exec nb-joinmarket capsh --print | grep Current",
            re.compile("^Current: =$", re.MULTILINE),
        )

      # chain-backend health check must keep passing across the restart — which
      # requires the public RPC whitelist to include getpeerinfo/getnodeaddresses
      # (lnd uses that RPC user). Removing either would flap lnd here.
      tests.lndSurvivesBitcoindRestart = true;
      test.extraTestScript = ''
        @test("lndSurvivesBitcoindRestart")
        def _():
            assert_running("lnd")
            machine.succeed("systemctl restart bitcoind.service")
            machine.wait_for_unit("bitcoind.service")
            machine.wait_until_succeeds("runuser -u operator -- bitcoin-cli getnetworkinfo")
            # Give lnd's chain-backend health check time to run a few cycles; it
            # self-shuts-down after 3 failures, so a missing whitelist entry
            # would take lnd down in this window.
            machine.sleep(45)
            assert_running("lnd")
            machine.fail("journalctl -u lnd | grep -q 'chain backend failed'")
      '';
    };

    # netns and regtest, without secure-node.nix

1:# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
2:
3:Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
4:
5:The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
6:that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
7:(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
8:interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
9:accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
10:The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
11:10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
12:things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
13:
14:> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
15:> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
16:> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
17:> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
18:
19:## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
20:
21:1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
22:   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
23:   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
24:   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
25:   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
26:   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
27:   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
28:   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
29:   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
30:   auditor trade-off of its own).
31:
32:2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
33:   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
34:   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
35:   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
36:   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
37:   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
38:   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
39:   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
40:   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
41:   must tolerate rotation.
42:
43:3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
44:   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
45:   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
46:   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
47:   the daemon is healthy.
48:
49:4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
50:   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
51:   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
52:   wg-dependent.
53:
54:5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
55:   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
56:   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
57:
58:6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
59:   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
60:
61:## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
62:
63:Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
64:the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
65:entries:
66:
67:```nix
68:services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
69:  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
70:'';
71:```
72:
73:All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
74:wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
75:targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
76:dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
77:
78:- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
79:  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
80:  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
81:- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
82:  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
83:  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
84:  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
85:  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
86:
87:## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
88:
89:- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
90:- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
91:      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
92:      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
93:      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
94:      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
95:      connects.
96:- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
97:- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
98:      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
99:      existing clients.
100:- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
101:      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
102:      items 1–6.

{
  options = {
    networking.firewall = {
      enable = lib.mkOption {
        type = lib.types.bool;
        default = true;
        description = ''
          Whether to enable the firewall.  This is a simple stateful
          firewall that blocks connection attempts to unauthorised TCP
          or UDP ports on this machine.
        '';
      };

      backend = lib.mkOption {
        type = lib.types.enum [
          "iptables"
          "nftables"
          "firewalld"
        ];
        default =
          if config.services.firewalld.enable then
            "firewalld"
          else if config.networking.nftables.enable then
            "nftables"
          else
            "iptables";
        defaultText = lib.literalExpression ''
          if config.services.firewalld.enable then
            "firewalld"
          else if config.networking.nftables.enable then
            "nftables"
          else
            "iptables"
        '';
        description = ''
          Underlying implementation for the firewall service.
        '';
      };

      package = lib.mkOption {
        type = lib.types.package;
        default = if config.networking.nftables.enable then pkgs.nftables else pkgs.iptables;
        defaultText = lib.literalExpression ''if config.networking.nftables.enable then "pkgs.nftables" else "pkgs.iptables"'';
        example = lib.literalExpression "pkgs.iptables-legacy";
        description = ''
          The package to use for running the firewall service.
        '';
    ${cfg.extraCommands}

    # Reject/drop everything else.
    ip46tables -A nixos-fw -j nixos-fw-log-refuse


    # Enable the firewall.
    ip46tables -A INPUT -j nixos-fw
  '';

  stopScript = writeShScript "firewall-stop" ''
    ${helpers}

    # Clean up in case reload fails
    ip46tables -D INPUT -j nixos-drop 2>/dev/null || true

    # Clean up after added ruleset
    ip46tables -D INPUT -j nixos-fw 2>/dev/null || true

    ${lib.optionalString (kernelHasRPFilter && (cfg.checkReversePath != false)) ''
      ip46tables -t mangle -D PREROUTING -j nixos-fw-rpfilter 2>/dev/null || true
    ''}

    ${cfg.extraStopCommands}
  '';

  reloadScript = writeShScript "firewall-reload" ''
    ${helpers}

    # Create a unique drop rule
    ip46tables -D INPUT -j nixos-drop 2>/dev/null || true
    ip46tables -F nixos-drop 2>/dev/null || true
    ip46tables -X nixos-drop 2>/dev/null || true
    ip46tables -N nixos-drop
    ip46tables -A nixos-drop -j DROP

    # Don't allow traffic to leak out until the script has completed
    ip46tables -A INPUT -j nixos-drop

    ${cfg.extraStopCommands}

    if ${startScript}; then
      ip46tables -D INPUT -j nixos-drop 2>/dev/null || true
    else
      echo "Failed to reload firewall... Stopping"
      ${stopScript}
      exit 1
    fi
  '';

{
  options = {
    networking.firewall = {
      extraCommands = lib.mkOption {
        type = lib.types.lines;
        default = "";
        example = "iptables -A INPUT -p icmp -j ACCEPT";
        description = ''
          Additional shell commands executed as part of the firewall
          initialisation script.  These are executed just before the
          final "reject" firewall rule is added, so they can be used
          to allow packets that would otherwise be refused.

          This option only works with the iptables based firewall.
        '';
      };

      extraStopCommands = lib.mkOption {
        type = lib.types.lines;
        default = "";
        example = "iptables -P INPUT ACCEPT";
        description = ''
          Additional shell commands executed as part of the firewall
          shutdown script.  These are executed just after the removal
          of the NixOS input rule, or if the service enters a failed
          state.

          This option only works with the iptables based firewall.
        '';
      };
    };
  };

  # FIXME: Maybe if `enable' is false, the firewall should still be
  # built but not started by default?
  config = lib.mkIf (cfg.enable && cfg.backend == "iptables") {
    assertions = [
      # This is approximately "checkReversePath -> kernelHasRPFilter",
      # but the checkReversePath option can include non-boolean
      # values.
      {
        assertion = cfg.checkReversePath == false || kernelHasRPFilter;
        message = "This kernel does not support rpfilter";
      }
    ];

    networking.firewall.checkReversePath = lib.mkIf (!kernelHasRPFilter) (lib.mkDefault false);

    environment.systemPackages = [ pkgs.nixos-firewall-tool ];

    systemd.services.firewall = {
      description = "Firewall";
      wantedBy = [ "sysinit.target" ];
      wants = [ "network-pre.target" ];
      after = [ "systemd-modules-load.service" ];
      before = [
        "network-pre.target"
        "shutdown.target"
      ];
      conflicts = [ "shutdown.target" ];

      path = [ cfg.package ] ++ cfg.extraPackages;

      # FIXME: this module may also try to load kernel modules, but
      # containers don't have CAP_SYS_MODULE.  So the host system had
      # better have all necessary modules already loaded.
      unitConfig.ConditionCapability = "CAP_NET_ADMIN";
      unitConfig.DefaultDependencies = false;

      reloadIfChanged = true;

      serviceConfig = {
        Type = "oneshot";
        RemainAfterExit = true;
        ExecStart = "@${startScript} firewall-start";
        ExecReload = "@${reloadScript} firewall-reload";
        ExecStop = "@${stopScript} firewall-stop";
      };
    };
  };

from collections import OrderedDict
import json
import re

def succeed(*cmds):
    """Returns the concatenated output of all cmds"""
    return machine.succeed(*cmds)

def assert_matches(cmd, regexp):
    assert_str_matches(succeed(cmd), regexp)

def assert_str_matches(str, regexp):
    if not re.search(regexp, str):
        raise Exception(f"Pattern '{regexp}' not found in '{str}'")

def assert_full_match(cmd, regexp):
    out = succeed(cmd)
    if not re.fullmatch(regexp, out):
        raise Exception(f"Pattern '{regexp}' doesn't match '{out}'")

def log_has_string(unit, str):
    return f"journalctl -b --output=cat -u {unit} --grep='{str}'"

def assert_no_failure(unit):
    """Unit should not have failed since the system is running"""
    machine.fail(log_has_string(unit, "Failed with result"))

def assert_running(unit):
    with machine.nested(f"waiting for unit: {unit}"):
        machine.wait_for_unit(unit)
    assert_no_failure(unit)

def wait_for_open_port(address, port):
    def is_port_open(_):
        status, _ = machine.execute(f"nc -z {address} {port}")
        return status == 0

    with machine.nested(f"Waiting for TCP port {address}:{port}"):
        retry(is_port_open)


### Test runner

tests = OrderedDict()

def test(name):
    def x(fn):
        tests[name] = fn
    return x

# `run_tests` is already defined by the NixOS test driver
def nb_run_tests():
    enabled = enabled_tests.copy()
    to_run = []
    for test in tests:
        if test in enabled:
            enabled.remove(test)
            to_run.append(test)
    if enabled:
        raise RuntimeError(f"The following tests are enabled but not defined: {enabled}")
    machine.connect()  # Visually separate boot output from the test output
    for test in to_run:
        with machine.nested(f"test: {test}"):
            tests[test]()

def run_test(test):
    tests[test]()


### Tests
# All tests are executed in the order they are defined here

@test("security")
def _():
    assert_running("setup-secrets")
    # Unused secrets should be inaccessible
    succeed('[[ $(stat -c "%U:%G %a" /secrets/dummy) = "root:root 440" ]]')

    if "secure-node" in enabled_tests:
        machine.wait_for_unit("bitcoind")
        # `systemctl status` run by unprivileged users shouldn't leak cgroup info
        assert_matches(
            "runuser -u electrs -- systemctl status bitcoind 2>&1 >/dev/null",
            "Failed to dump process list for 'bitcoind.service', ignoring: Access denied",
        )
        # The 'operator' with group 'proc' has full access
        assert_full_match("runuser -u operator -- systemctl status bitcoind 2>&1 >/dev/null", "")

@test("bitcoind")
def _():
    assert_running("bitcoind")
    machine.wait_until_succeeds("bitcoin-cli getnetworkinfo")
    assert_matches("runuser -u operator -- bitcoin-cli getnetworkinfo | jq", '"version"')

    regtest = "regtest/" if "regtest" in enabled_tests else ""
    assert_full_match(f"stat  -c '%a' /var/lib/bitcoind/{regtest}.cookie", "640\n")

    # RPC access for user 'public' should be restricted
    machine.fail(
        "bitcoin-cli -rpcuser=public -rpcpassword=$(cat /secrets/bitcoin-rpcpassword-public) stop"
    )
    machine.wait_until_succeeds(
        log_has_string("bitcoind", "RPC User public not allowed to call method stop")
    )

            bitcoind = super.bitcoind.overrideAttrs (old: {
              # dostuff

      };

        readOnly = true;
        default = import ../pkgs/lib.nix lib pkgs config;
        defaultText = "nix-bitcoin/pkgs/lib.nix";

      torClientAddressWithPort = mkOption {
        readOnly = true;
        default = with config.services.tor.client.socksListenAddress;
          "${addr}:${toString port}";
        defaultText = "(See source)";
      };

      # Torify binary that works with custom Tor SOCKS addresses
      # Related issue: https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs/issues/94236
      torify = mkOption {
        readOnly = true;
        default = pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "torify" ''
          ${pkgs.tor}/bin/torify \
            --address ${config.services.tor.client.socksListenAddress.addr} \
            "$@"
        '';
        defaultText = "(See source)";
      };

      # A helper for using doas instead of sudo when doas is enabled
      runAsUserCmd = mkOption {
        readOnly = true;
        default = if config.security.doas.enable
                  then "doas -u"
                  else "sudo -u";
        defaultText = "(See source)";
      };
    };
  };

) -> Result<()> {
    let start_list = scope.start_list_file();
    let restart_list = scope.restart_list_file();
    let reload_list = scope.reload_list_file();
    let use_restart_as_stop_and_start = new_unit_info.is_none();

    if cannot_be_restarted_directly(unit) {
        // Do nothing.  These cannot be restarted directly.

        // Slices and Paths don't have to be restarted since properties (resource limits and
        // inotify watches) seem to get applied on daemon-reload.
    } else if unit.ends_with(".mount") {
        // Just restart the unit. We wouldn't have gotten into this subroutine if only `Options`
        // was changed, in which case the unit would be reloaded. The only exception is / and /nix
        // because it's very unlikely we can safely unmount them so we reload them instead. This
        // means that we may not get all changes into the running system but it's better than
        // crashing it.
        if unit == "-.mount" || unit == "nix.mount" {
            units_to_reload.insert(unit.to_string(), ());
            record_unit(&reload_list, unit);
        } else {
            units_to_restart.insert(unit.to_string(), ());
            record_unit(&restart_list, unit);
        }
    } else if unit.ends_with(".socket") {
        // FIXME: do something?
        // Attempt to fix this: https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs/pull/141192
        // Revert of the attempt: https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs/pull/147609
        // More details: https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs/issues/74899#issuecomment-981142430
    } else {
        let fallback = parse_unit(new_unit_file, new_base_unit_file)?;
        let new_unit_info = if new_unit_info.is_some() {
            new_unit_info
        } else {
            Some(&fallback)
        };

        if parse_systemd_bool(new_unit_info, "Service", "X-ReloadIfChanged", false)
            && !units_to_restart.contains_key(unit)
            && !(if use_restart_as_stop_and_start {
                units_to_restart.contains_key(unit)
            } else {
                units_to_stop.contains_key(unit)
            })
        {
            units_to_reload.insert(unit.to_string(), ());
            record_unit(&reload_list, unit);
        } else if !parse_systemd_bool(new_unit_info, "Service", "X-RestartIfChanged", true)
            || parse_systemd_bool(new_unit_info, "Unit", "RefuseManualStop", false)
            || parse_systemd_bool(new_unit_info, "Unit", "X-OnlyManualStart", false)
        {
            units_to_skip.insert(unit.to_string(), ());
        } else {
            // It doesn't make sense to stop and start non-services because they can't have
            // ExecStop=
            if !parse_systemd_bool(new_unit_info, "Service", "X-StopIfChanged", true)
                || !unit.ends_with(".service")
            {
                // This unit should be restarted instead of stopped and started.
                units_to_restart.insert(unit.to_string(), ());
                record_unit(&restart_list, unit);
                // Remove from units to reload so we don't restart and reload
                if units_to_reload.contains_key(unit) {
                    units_to_reload.remove(unit);
                    unrecord_unit(&reload_list, unit);
                }
            } else {
                // If this unit is socket-activated, then stop the socket unit(s) as well, and
                // restart the socket(s) instead of the service.
                // We count as "socket-activated" any unit that doesn't declare itself not so
                // via X-NotSocketActivated, that has any associated .socket units.
        .filter(|(unit, unit_state)| {
            let Ok(fragment_path) = unit_state
                .proxy
                .get::<String>("org.freedesktop.systemd1.Unit", "FragmentPath")
            else {
                return false;
            };
            let fragment_parent = Path::new(&fragment_path).parent();

            // Already in /etc: handled by pass 1.
            if fragment_parent == Some(fragment_dir) {
                return false;
            }

            // Loaded from ~/.config/systemd/user, which shadows /etc.
            if let Some(dir) = &user_config_unit_dir {
                if fragment_parent == Some(dir.as_path()) {
                    return true;
                }
            }

            // Elsewhere: only act if /etc is gaining the unit this switch.
            !old_unit_dir.join(unit).exists()
        })
        .map(|(unit, _)| unit.clone())
        .collect();

    collect_unit_changes(
        &toplevel,
        scope,
        &old_unit_dir,
        &new_unit_dir,
        &current_active_units,
        &mut units_to_stop,
        &mut units_to_start,
        &mut units_to_reload,
        &mut units_to_restart,
        &mut units_to_skip,
        &mut units_to_filter,
    )?;

    // Restarted unconditionally below; don't list it as skipped.
    units_to_skip.remove("nixos-activation.service");

    let print_units = |verb: &str, units: &HashMap<String, ()>| {
        if units.is_empty() {
            return;
        }
        let mut names: Vec<&str> = units.keys().map(String::as_str).collect();
        names.sort_by_key(|n| n.to_lowercase());
        eprintln!("{verb} the following user units: {}", names.join(", "));
    };

    if dry_run {
        print_units("would stop", &units_to_stop);
        if !units_to_skip.is_empty() {
            print_units("would NOT restart", &units_to_skip);
        }
        print_units("would reload", &units_to_reload);
        print_units("would restart", &units_to_restart);
        print_units(
            "would start",
            &filter_units(&units_to_filter, &units_to_start),
        );
        return Ok(());
    }

    let mut exit_code = 0;

    // Stop units before reexec so that ExecStop runs with the old definition.
    print_units("stopping", &units_to_stop);
    for unit in units_to_stop.keys() {
        if let Ok(job_path) = systemd.stop_unit(unit, "replace") {
            submitted_jobs.borrow_mut().insert(job_path, Job::Stop);
        }
    }
    block_on_jobs(&dbus_conn, &submitted_jobs)?;

    if !units_to_skip.is_empty() {
        print_units("NOT restarting", &units_to_skip);
    }

    // The systemd user session seems to not send a Reloaded signal, so we don't have anything to
    // wait on here.
    _ = systemd.reexecute();

    // Reset failed and reload so that subsequent starts use the new unit files.
    _ = systemd.reset_failed();
    _ = systemd.reload();

    print_units("reloading", &units_to_reload);
    for unit in units_to_reload.keys() {
        match systemd.reload_unit(unit, "replace") {
            Ok(job_path) => {
                submitted_jobs.borrow_mut().insert(job_path, Job::Reload);
            }
            Err(err) => {
                eprintln!("Failed to reload user unit {unit}: {err}");
                exit_code = 4;
            }
        }
    }
    block_on_jobs(&dbus_conn, &submitted_jobs)?;
    remove_file_if_exists(&reload_list)
        .with_context(|| format!("Failed to remove {}", reload_list.display()))?;

24:    enable = lib.mkEnableOption "FirewallD";
42:  config = lib.mkIf cfg.enable {

    // We can remove the file now because it has been propagated to the other restart/reload files

        .with_context(|| format!("Failed to remove {RESTART_BY_ACTIVATION_LIST_FILE}"))?;

    for unit in std::fs::read_to_string(RELOAD_BY_ACTIVATION_LIST_FILE)
        .unwrap_or_default()

            && !units_to_restart.contains_key(unit)

    // We can remove the file now because it has been propagated to the other reload file
    remove_file_if_exists(RELOAD_BY_ACTIVATION_LIST_FILE)

    }

    // Restart sysinit-reactivation.target. This target only exists to restart services ordered
    // before sysinit.target. We cannot use X-StopOnReconfiguration to restart sysinit.target
    // because then ALL services of the system would be restarted since all normal services have a
    // default dependency on sysinit.target. sysinit-reactivation.target ensures that services
    // ordered BEFORE sysinit.target get re-started in the correct order. Ordering between these
    // services is respected.
    eprintln!("restarting {SYSINIT_REACTIVATION_TARGET}");
    match systemd.restart_unit(SYSINIT_REACTIVATION_TARGET, "replace") {
        Ok(job_path) => {
            let mut jobs = submitted_jobs.borrow_mut();
            jobs.insert(job_path, Job::Restart);
        }
        Err(err) => {
            eprintln!("Failed to restart {SYSINIT_REACTIVATION_TARGET}: {err}");
            exit_code = 4;
        }
    }

    // Wait for the restart job of sysinit-reactivation.service to finish
    block_on_jobs(&dbus_conn, &submitted_jobs)?;

    // Before reloading we need to ensure that the units are still active. They may have been
    // deactivated because one of their requirements got stopped. If they are inactive but should
    // have been reloaded, the user probably expects them to be started.
    if !units_to_reload.is_empty() {
        for (unit, _) in units_to_reload.clone() {
            if !unit_is_active(&dbus_conn, &unit)? {
                // Figure out if we need to start the unit. We skip units that are not found in the
                // NixOS-managed /etc/systemd/system directory (e.g. mount units that are generated
                // from /etc/fstab).
                if parse_unit(
                    toplevel.join("etc/systemd/system").join(&unit).as_path(),
                    toplevel.join("etc/systemd/system").join(&unit).as_path(),
                )
                .map(|unit_info| {
                    !parse_systemd_bool(Some(&unit_info), "Unit", "RefuseManualStart", false)
                        || parse_systemd_bool(Some(&unit_info), "Unit", "X-OnlyManualStart", false)
                })
                .unwrap_or_default()
                {
                    units_to_start.insert(unit.clone(), ());
                    record_unit(START_LIST_FILE, &unit);
                }

                // Don't reload the unit, reloading would fail
                units_to_reload.remove(&unit);
                unrecord_unit(RELOAD_LIST_FILE, &unit);
            }
        }
    }

    // Reload units that need it. This includes remounting changed mount units.
    if !units_to_reload.is_empty() {
        let mut units = units_to_reload
            .keys()
            .map(String::as_str)
            .collect::<Vec<&str>>();
        units.sort_by_key(|name| name.to_lowercase());
        eprintln!("reloading the following units: {}", units.join(", "));

        for unit in units {
            match systemd.reload_unit(unit, "replace") {
                Ok(job_path) => {
                    submitted_jobs
                        .borrow_mut()
                        .insert(job_path.clone(), Job::Reload);
                }
                Err(err) => {
                    eprintln!("Failed to reload {unit}: {err}");
                    exit_code = 4;
                }
            }
        }

        block_on_jobs(&dbus_conn, &submitted_jobs)?;

        remove_file_if_exists(RELOAD_LIST_FILE)
            .with_context(|| format!("Failed to remove {RELOAD_LIST_FILE}"))?;
    }

    // Restart changed services (those that have to be restarted rather than stopped and started).
    if !units_to_restart.is_empty() {
        let mut units = units_to_restart
docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md:139:| M-3 public RPC whitelist | FIXED | Dropped the DoS/side-effect methods (scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate, getblockfrompeer); whitelist now mkDefault-overridable. getpeerinfo/getnodeaddresses are KEPT — the "public" user is also lnd/nbxplorer's local RPC user and they require them (removing them fails lnd's chain-backend health check). codex
The stale FORWARD rule is real, but FW-3 misstates the mechanism: NixOS reloads the firewall with the new configuration, whose cleanup command has disappeared. FW-2’s proposed tests also need explicit routing and service readiness checks; otherwise a failed request could reflect a missing route or a service that has not started.

/bin/bash -lc "python3 -B - <<'PY'
import subprocess, base64, struct, hashlib, collections
try:
    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.ed25519 import Ed25519PublicKey
except ImportError:
    print('No in-memory signature verification library available')
    raise SystemExit

def field(data, i):
    n=struct.unpack('>I', data[i:i+4])[0]
    return data[i+4:i+4+n], i+4+n
s=lambda b:struct.pack('>I',len(b))+b
results=collections.Counter()
for rev in subprocess.check_output(['git','rev-list','37931e5..HEAD'],text=True).splitlines():
    obj=subprocess.check_output(['git','cat-file','commit',rev])
    start=obj.index(b'gpgsig -----BEGIN SSH SIGNATURE-----\\n')
    end=obj.index(b' -----END SSH SIGNATURE-----\\n',start)+len(b' -----END SSH SIGNATURE-----\\n')
    encoded=obj[start:end].split(b'\\n')[1:-2]
    sig=base64.b64decode(b''.join(encoded))
    pub,i=field(sig,10)
    ns,i=field(sig,i)
    reserved,i=field(sig,i)
    alg,i=field(sig,i)
    signature,i=field(sig,i)
    pubalg,j=field(pub,0)
    key,j=field(pub,j)
    sigalg,j=field(signature,0)
    sigbytes,j=field(signature,j)
    payload=obj[:start]+obj[end:]
    signed=b'SSHSIG'+s(ns)+s(reserved)+s(alg)+s(hashlib.new(alg.decode(),payload).digest())
    try:
        Ed25519PublicKey.from_public_bytes(key).verify(sigbytes,signed)
        results['cryptographically valid SSH signatures']+=1
    except Exception as e:
        results[str(type(e).__name__)]+=1
print(dict(results))

@test("lnd-macaroon-read-deprivileged")
def _():
    # Regression test for the 2026-08-30 audit finding (modules/lnd.nix): the
    # root ExecStartPost that mints custom macaroons must read admin.macaroon AS
    # THE LND USER, never as root. admin.macaroon sits in lnd's own 0770 datadir,
    # so a compromised lnd can replace it with a symlink to a root-only file; if
    # root dereferences it, the bytes are exfiltrated to lnd via the REST call.
    # Reading as the lnd user fails closed on such a symlink.
    assert_running("lnd")

    # (a) Pin the deployed unit: the macaroon read must go through the run-as-lnd
    #     wrapper. Reverting the fix to a bare root `xxd ... admin.macaroon`
    #     makes this grep fail.
    script = succeed(
        "systemctl show lnd -p ExecStartPost --value | "
        "grep -oE '/nix/store/[a-z0-9]+-lnd-create-macaroons' | head -1"
    ).strip()
    assert script, "lnd-create-macaroons ExecStartPost script not found"
    assert_matches(
        f"cat {script}",
        r"runuser -u lnd [^\n]*xxd[^\n]*admin\.macaroon",
    )

    # (b) Prove the runtime asymmetry the fix relies on, in lnd's real datadir,
    #     as the real lnd user, without disrupting the service. The canary sits
    #     in a traversable dir (/run) but is root-only readable, mirroring the
    #     real targets (backup passphrase, wg key, host keys).
    succeed(
        "printf ROOT_ONLY_CANARY > /run/macaroon-canary",
        "chown root:root /run/macaroon-canary",
        "chmod 400 /run/macaroon-canary",
    )
    link = "/var/lib/lnd/attack-test-link"
    # The attacker is the lnd user, which owns its 0770 datadir.
    succeed(f"runuser -u lnd -- ln -sf /run/macaroon-canary {link}")
    # Root WOULD read the canary through the symlink (the exploit precondition).
    assert_matches(f"cat {link}", "ROOT_ONLY_CANARY")
    # The lnd user must NOT be able to — so the fix's read fails closed.
    machine.fail(f"runuser -u lnd -- cat {link}")
    succeed(f"rm -f {link} /run/macaroon-canary")

@test("lndconnect-onion-lnd")
